# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
A company is comprised of two to eight departments. Each department has an ID and email. A department hires employees for certain projects. Employees working on projects can be temporary employees or permanent employees. Each employee is identified by a name, email, employee ID, employee number. Projects can be of types - production projects, research projects, education projects, and community projects. All projects have a title, description, budget amount, and deadline. In addition, each education project and community project are associated with one funding group. The funding group can be of types - private group, government group, or mixed group. The production projects are characterized by a site code.

functional requirement：
1.Total budget of all projects in a company.Calculate the sum of the budget amounts of all projects in all departments of a company. 

2.Average budget of all projects in a department.Calculate the average budget amount of all projects in a given department.

3.Count the distinct number of employees who work on at least one production projects in the company. An employee working on multiple production projects is counted only once.

4.Funding group type of all community projects in a department.Retrieve the funding group type of all community projects within a given department.

5.Manage departments in a company. Ensure that the company always has between 2 and 8 departments (inclusive). Allow adding a new department with unique name only if the current number of departments is less than 8, and allow removing an existing department by name only if the current number of departments is greater than 2; reject any operation that would violate these constraints. When the number of departments exceeds the maximum limit or falls below the minimum limit, throw an IllegalStateException; when the department name is duplicate, null, empty, or the target department to remove does not exist, throw an IllegalArgumentException.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title project Model

class CommunityProject {
    fundingGroup : FundingGroup
}
class Company {
    departments : List<Department>
    addDepartment(department : Department) : void
    calculateTotalBudget() : double
    countEmployeesInProductionProjects() : int
    removeDepartment(name : String) : String
}
class Department {
    ID : String
    email : String
    company : Company
    employees : List<Employee>
    projects : List<Project>
    calculateAverageBudget() : double
    getFundingGroupTypeCommunityProjects() : List<FundingGroupType>
}
class EducationProject {
    fundingGroup : FundingGroup
}
class Employee {
    ID : String
    email : String
    name : String
    number : String
    type : EmployeeType
    department : Department
}
class FundingGroup {
    name : String
    type : FundingGroupType
    communityProjects : List<CommunityProject>
    educationProjects : List<EducationProject>
}
class ProductionProject {
    siteCode : String
}
abstract class Project {
    budget : double
    deadline : EDate
    description : String
    title : String
    department : Department
    workingEmployees : List<Employee>
}
class ResearchProject {
}
enum EmployeeType {
    TEMPORARY
    PERMANENT
}
enum FundingGroupType {
    PRIVATE
    GOVERNMENT
    MIXED
}

CommunityProject --|> Project
CommunityProject --> FundingGroup : fundingGroup  'opposite: communityProjects
Company *-- "*" Department : departments  'opposite: company
Department --> Company : company  'opposite: departments
Department *-- "*" Employee : employees  'opposite: department
Department *-- "*" Project : projects  'opposite: department
EducationProject --|> Project
EducationProject --> FundingGroup : fundingGroup  'opposite: educationProjects
Employee --> Department : department  'opposite: employees
FundingGroup --> "*" CommunityProject : communityProjects  'opposite: fundingGroup
FundingGroup --> "*" EducationProject : educationProjects  'opposite: fundingGroup
ProductionProject --|> Project
Project --> Department : department  'opposite: projects
Project --> "*" Employee : workingEmployees
ResearchProject --|> Project
@enduml
```

# Requirement
```text
A company is comprised of two to eight departments. Each department has an ID and email. A department hires employees for certain projects. Employees working on projects can be temporary employees or permanent employees. Each employee is identified by a name, email, employee ID, employee number. Projects can be of types - production projects, research projects, education projects, and community projects. All projects have a title, description, budget amount, and deadline. In addition, each education project and community project are associated with one funding group. The funding group can be of types - private group, government group, or mixed group. The production projects are characterized by a site code.

functional requirement：
1.Total budget of all projects in a company.Calculate the sum of the budget amounts of all projects in all departments of a company. 

2.Average budget of all projects in a department.Calculate the average budget amount of all projects in a given department.

3.Count the distinct number of employees who work on at least one production projects in the company. An employee working on multiple production projects is counted only once.

4.Funding group type of all community projects in a department.Retrieve the funding group type of all community projects within a given department.

5.Manage departments in a company. Ensure that the company always has between 2 and 8 departments (inclusive). Allow adding a new department with unique name only if the current number of departments is less than 8, and allow removing an existing department by name only if the current number of departments is greater than 2; reject any operation that would violate these constraints. When the number of departments exceeds the maximum limit or falls below the minimum limit, throw an IllegalStateException; when the department name is duplicate, null, empty, or the target department to remove does not exist, throw an IllegalArgumentException.
```

   - Let `projectsList = this.projects`.
   - If `projectsList.size() == 0`, return 0.0.
   - Initialize `sum = 0.0`.
   - For each `proj` in `projectsList`: `sum += proj.budget`.
   - Return `sum / projectsList.size()`.

5. Input: None
6. Output: double - Average budget amount. Value range: ≥ 0.0.
7. Pre-condition: Department instance is initialized; `projects` list exists.
8. Post-condition: Returns the calculated average. System state remains completely unchanged.

# Operation Specifications
Company::addDepartment
1. Summary: Adds a new department to the company while enforcing constraints on the maximum department count (≤8) and department name uniqueness.
2. Algorithm: 
   - If `department` is null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
   - If `department.name` is null or empty, throw IllegalArgumentException.
   - If `departments.size() >= 8`, throw IllegalStateException.
   - If any existing department in `departments` has the same name as `department.name`, throw IllegalArgumentException.
   - Otherwise, append `department` to `departments` and assign `department.company = this`.
3. Related Features: Company.departments, Department.name (inferred), Department.company.
4. Related Classes: Department
5. Input: department : Department - The department instance to be added. Must contain a valid, non-null, non-empty name.
6. Output: void
7. Pre-condition: Company instance is fully constructed; `departments` list is initialized and mutable.
8. Post-condition: On success, `departments` contains the new department and its cardinality increases by 1; `department.company` references this Company. On constraint violation, no state changes occur and respective exceptions are thrown.

Company::removeDepartment
1. Summary: Removes an existing department from the company by name, enforcing the minimum department count (≥2) and existence checks.
2. Algorithm: 
   - If `name` is null or empty, throw IllegalArgumentException.
   - Search `departments` for a department matching `name`. If not found, throw IllegalArgumentException.
   - If `departments.size() <= 2`, throw IllegalStateException.
   - If valid, locate the department, remove it from `departments`, set its `company` reference to null, and return "Department removed successfully".
3. Related Features: Company.departments, Department.name (inferred), Department.company.
4. Related Classes: Department
5. Input: name : String - Name of the department to remove. Must be non-null and non-empty.
6. Output: String - Status message indicating successful removal.
7. Pre-condition: Company instance is active; `departments` list is initialized.
8. Post-condition: On success, the specified department is detached and removed from the list, reducing cardinality by 1. State remains unchanged if constraints are violated (exceptions thrown).

Company::calculateTotalBudget
1. Summary: Computes the aggregate budget amount across all projects belonging to all departments in the company.
2. Algorithm: 
   - Initialize `totalBudget = 0.0`.
   - For each `dept` in `departments`:
     - For each `proj` in `dept.projects`:
       - `totalBudget += proj.budget`.
   - Return `totalBudget`.
3. Related Features: Company.departments, Department.projects, Project.budget.
4. Related Classes: Department, Project
5. Input: None
6. Output: double - Total budget amount. Value range: ≥ 0.0.
7. Pre-condition: Company and its `departments` list are initialized.
8. Post-condition: Returns the precise sum of all project budgets. System state remains completely unchanged.

Company::countEmployeesInProductionProjects
1. Summary: Counts the number of unique employees assigned to at least one production project across the entire company.
2. Algorithm: 
   - Initialize a HashSet `distinctEmps`.
   - For each `dept` in `departments`:
     - For each `proj` in `dept.projects`:
       - If `proj` is an instance of ProductionProject:
         - For each `emp` in `proj.workingEmployees`:
           - Add `emp` to `distinctEmps`.
   - Return `distinctEmps.size()`.
3. Related Features: Company.departments, Department.projects, Project.workingEmployees, Runtime Type Identification (RTTI) for ProductionProject.
4. Related Classes: Department, Project, ProductionProject, Employee
5. Input: None
6. Output: int - Number of distinct employees. Value range: ≥ 0.
7. Pre-condition: Company and its `departments` list are initialized.
8. Post-condition: Returns the exact count of unique employees working on production projects. System state remains completely unchanged.

You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;

6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

# Requirement
```text
A company is comprised of two to eight departments. Each department has an ID and email. A department hires employees for certain projects. Employees working on projects can be temporary employees or permanent employees. Each employee is identified by a name, email, employee ID, employee number. Projects can be of types - production projects, research projects, education projects, and community projects. All projects have a title, description, budget amount, and deadline. In addition, each education project and community project are associated with one funding group. The funding group can be of types - private group, government group, or mixed group. The production projects are characterized by a site code.

functional requirement：
1.Total budget of all projects in a company.Calculate the sum of the budget amounts of all projects in all departments of a company. 

2.Average budget of all projects in a department.Calculate the average budget amount of all projects in a given department.

3.Count the distinct number of employees who work on at least one production projects in the company. An employee working on multiple production projects is counted only once.

4.Funding group type of all community projects in a department.Retrieve the funding group type of all community projects within a given department.

5.Manage departments in a company. Ensure that the company always has between 2 and 8 departments (inclusive). Allow adding a new department with unique name only if the current number of departments is less than 8, and allow removing an existing department by name only if the current number of departments is greater than 2; reject any operation that would violate these constraints. When the number of departments exceeds the maximum limit or falls below the minimum limit, throw an IllegalStateException; when the department name is duplicate, null, empty, or the target department to remove does not exist, throw an IllegalArgumentException.
```

# Operation Specifications
Company::addDepartment
1. Summary: Adds a new department to the company while enforcing constraints on the maximum department count (≤8) and department name uniqueness.
2. Algorithm: 
   - If `department` is null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
   - If `department.name` is null or empty, throw IllegalArgumentException.
   - If `departments.size() >= 8`, throw IllegalStateException.
   - If any existing department in `departments` has the same name as `department.name`, throw IllegalArgumentException.
   - Otherwise, append `department` to `departments` and assign `department.company = this`.
3. Related Features: Company.departments, Department.name (inferred), Department.company.
4. Related Classes: Department
5. Input: department : Department - The department instance to be added. Must contain a valid, non-null, non-empty name.
6. Output: void
7. Pre-condition: Company instance is fully constructed; `departments` list is initialized and mutable.
8. Post-condition: On success, `departments` contains the new department and its cardinality increases by 1; `department.company` references this Company. On constraint violation, no state changes occur and respective exceptions are thrown.

Company::removeDepartment
1. Summary: Removes an existing department from the company by name, enforcing the minimum department count (≥2) and existence checks.
2. Algorithm: 
   - If `name` is null or empty, throw IllegalArgumentException.
   - Search `departments` for a department matching `name`. If not found, throw IllegalArgumentException.
   - If `departments.size() <= 2`, throw IllegalStateException.
   - If valid, locate the department, remove it from `departments`, set its `company` reference to null, and return "Department removed successfully".
3. Related Features: Company.departments, Department.name (inferred), Department.company.
4. Related Classes: Department
5. Input: name : String - Name of the department to remove. Must be non-null and non-empty.
6. Output: String - Status message indicating successful removal.
7. Pre-condition: Company instance is active; `departments` list is initialized.
8. Post-condition: On success, the specified department is detached and removed from the list, reducing cardinality by 1. State remains unchanged if constraints are violated (exceptions thrown).

Company::calculateTotalBudget
1. Summary: Computes the aggregate budget amount across all projects belonging to all departments in the company.
2. Algorithm: 
   - Initialize `totalBudget = 0.0`.
   - For each `dept` in `departments`:
     - For each `proj` in `dept.projects`:
       - `totalBudget += proj.budget`.
   - Return `totalBudget`.
3. Related Features: Company.departments, Department.projects, Project.budget.
4. Related Classes: Department, Project
5. Input: None
6. Output: double - Total budget amount. Value range: ≥ 0.0.
7. Pre-condition: Company and its `departments` list are initialized.
8. Post-condition: Returns the precise sum of all project budgets. System state remains completely unchanged.

Company::countEmployeesInProductionProjects
1. Summary: Counts the number of unique employees assigned to at least one production project across the entire company.
2. Algorithm: 
   - Initialize a HashSet `distinctEmps`.
   - For each `dept` in `departments`:
     - For each `proj` in `dept.projects`:
       - If `proj` is an instance of ProductionProject:
         - For each `emp` in `proj.workingEmployees`:
           - Add `emp` to `distinctEmps`.
   - Return `distinctEmps.size()`.
3. Related Features: Company.departments, Department.projects, Project.workingEmployees, Runtime Type Identification (RTTI) for ProductionProject.
4. Related Classes: Department, Project, ProductionProject, Employee
5. Input: None
6. Output: int - Number of distinct employees. Value range: ≥ 0.
7. Pre-condition: Company and its `departments` list are initialized.
8. Post-condition: Returns the exact count of unique employees working on production projects. System state remains completely unchanged.

  public double calculateAverageBudget()

    EList<Project> projectsList = getProjects();
    if (projectsList.isEmpty()) {

    }

  public void addDepartment(final Department department)
  {
    if (department == null)
    {
      throw new IllegalArgumentException("Department cannot be null.");
    }
    String deptName = department.getName();
    if (deptName == null || deptName.isEmpty())
    {
      throw new IllegalArgumentException("Department name cannot be null or empty.");
    }
    if (getDepartments().size() >= 8)
    {
      throw new IllegalStateException("Maximum number of departments (8) reached.");

      {

      }
    }
    getDepartments().add(department);
    department.setCompany(this);
  }

    for (Department dept : getDepartments())

  1.2 Locate these files in your project.
  1.3 Generate the class summarization.
2. Read the error messages carefully.
3. Do your best to fix the errors locally without introducing new types, fields, and methods.
4. You should not remove faulty methods to eliminate compilation errors.
5. Do not change parts without errors.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * 1. Summary: Adds a new department to the company while enforcing constraints on the maximum department count (≤8) and department name uniqueness.
   * 2. Algorithm: 
   *    - If `department` is null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
   *    - If `department.name` is null or empty, throw IllegalArgumentException.
   *    - If `departments.size() >= 8`, throw IllegalStateException.
   *    - If any existing department in `departments` has the same name as `department.name`, throw IllegalArgumentException.
   *    - Otherwise, append `department` to `departments` and assign `department.company = this`.
   * 3. Related Features: Company.departments, Department.name (inferred), Department.company.
   * 4. Related Classes: Department
   * 5. Input: department : Department - The department instance to be added. Must contain a valid, non-null, non-empty name.
   * 6. Output: void
   * 7. Pre-condition: Company instance is fully constructed; `departments` list is initialized and mutable.
   * 8. Post-condition: On success, `departments` contains the new department and its cardinality increases by 1; `department.company` references this Company. On constraint violation, no state changes occur and respective exceptions are thrown.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public void addDepartment(final Department department)
  {
    if (department == null)
    {
      throw new IllegalArgumentException("Department cannot be null.");
    }
    String deptName = department.getName();
    if (deptName == null || deptName.isEmpty())
    {
      throw new IllegalArgumentException("Department name cannot be null or empty.");
    }
    if (getDepartments().size() >= 8)
    {
      throw new IllegalStateException("Maximum number of departments (8) reached.");
    }
    for (Department existingDept : getDepartments())
    {
      if (deptName.equals(existingDept.getName()))
      {
        throw new IllegalArgumentException("A department with this name already exists.");
      }
    }
    getDepartments().add(department);
    department.setCompany(this);
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * 1. Summary: Removes an existing department from the company by name, enforcing the minimum department count (≥2) and existence checks.
   * 2. Algorithm: 
   *    - If `name` is null or empty, throw IllegalArgumentException.
   *    - Search `departments` for a department matching `name`. If not found, throw IllegalArgumentException.
   *    - If `departments.size() <= 2`, throw IllegalStateException.
   *    - If valid, locate the department, remove it from `departments`, set its `company` reference to null, and return "Department removed successfully".
   * 3. Related Features: Company.departments, Department.name (inferred), Department.company.
   * 4. Related Classes: Department
   * 5. Input: name : String - Name of the department to remove. Must be non-null and non-empty.
   * 6. Output: String - Status message indicating successful removal.
   * 7. Pre-condition: Company instance is active; `departments` list is initialized.
   * 8. Post-condition: On success, the specified department is detached and removed from the list, reducing cardinality by 1. State remains unchanged if constraints are violated (exceptions thrown).
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public String removeDepartment(final String name)
  {
    if (name == null || name.isEmpty())
    {
      throw new IllegalArgumentException("Name cannot be null or empty.");
    }
    Department targetDept = null;
    for (Department dept : getDepartments())
    {
      if (name.equals(dept.getName()))
      {
        targetDept = dept;
        break;
      }
    }
    if (targetDept == null)
    {
      throw new IllegalArgumentException("Department not found.");
    }
    if (getDepartments().size() <= 2)
    {
      throw new IllegalStateException("Minimum number of departments (2) must be maintained.");
    }
    getDepartments().remove(targetDept);
    targetDept.setCompany(null);
    return "Department removed successfully";
  }

   * 2. Algorithm: 
   *    - If `department` is null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
   *    - If `department.name` is null or empty, throw IllegalArgumentException.
   *    - If `departments.size() >= 8`, throw IllegalStateException.
   *    - If any existing department in `departments` has the same name as `department.name`, throw IllegalArgumentException.
   *    - Otherwise, append `department` to `departments` and assign `department.company = this`.
   * 3. Related Features: Company.departments, Department.name (inferred), Department.company.
   * 4. Related Classes: Department
   * 5. Input: department : Department - The department instance to be added. Must contain a valid, non-null, non-empty name.
   * 6. Output: void
   * 7. Pre-condition: Company instance is fully constructed; `departments` list is initialized and mutable.
   * 8. Post-condition: On success, `departments` contains the new department and its cardinality increases by 1; `department.company` references this Company. On constraint violation, no state changes occur and respective exceptions are thrown.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public void addDepartment(final Department department)
  {
    if (department == null)
    {
      throw new IllegalArgumentException("Department cannot be null.");
    }
    String deptName = department.getName();
    if (deptName == null || deptName.isEmpty())
    {
      throw new IllegalArgumentException("Department name cannot be null or empty.");
    }
    if (getDepartments().size() >= 8)
    {
      throw new IllegalStateException("Maximum number of departments (8) reached.");
    }
    for (Department existingDept : getDepartments())
    {
      if (deptName.equals(existingDept.getName()))
      {
        throw new IllegalArgumentException("A department with this name already exists.");
      }
    }
    getDepartments().add(department);
    department.setCompany(this);
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * 1. Summary: Removes an existing department from the company by name, enforcing the minimum department count (≥2) and existence checks.
   * 2. Algorithm: 
   *    - If `name` is null or empty, throw IllegalArgumentException.
   *    - Search `departments` for a department matching `name`. If not found, throw IllegalArgumentException.
   *    - If `departments.size() <= 2`, throw IllegalStateException.
   *    - If valid, locate the department, remove it from `departments`, set its `company` reference to null, and return "Department removed successfully".
   * 3. Related Features: Company.departments, Department.name (inferred), Department.company.
   * 4. Related Classes: Department
   * 5. Input: name : String - Name of the department to remove. Must be non-null and non-empty.
   * 6. Output: String - Status message indicating successful removal.
   * 7. Pre-condition: Company instance is active; `departments` list is initialized.
   * 8. Post-condition: On success, the specified department is detached and removed from the list, reducing cardinality by 1. State remains unchanged if constraints are violated (exceptions thrown).
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public String removeDepartment(final String name)
  {
    if (name == null || name.isEmpty())
    {
      throw new IllegalArgumentException("Name cannot be null or empty.");
    }
    Department targetDept = null;
    for (Department dept : getDepartments())
    {
      if (name.equals(dept.getName()))
      {
        targetDept = dept;
        break;
      }
    }
    if (targetDept == null)
    {
      throw new IllegalArgumentException("Department not found.");
    }
    if (getDepartments().size() <= 2)
    {
      throw new IllegalStateException("Minimum number of departments (2) must be maintained.");
    }
    getDepartments().remove(targetDept);
    targetDept.setCompany(null);
    return "Department removed successfully";
  }

You will be given a **Java Class** that is originally generated by EMF code generator with `suppressInterfaces=true` and later is completed by LLMs. Your task is to fix the **Compilation Errors** by taking additional contextual information into account.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.2 Locate these files in your project.
  1.3 Generate the class summarization.
2. Read the error messages carefully.
3. Do your best to fix the errors locally without introducing new types, fields, and methods.
4. You should not remove faulty methods to eliminate compilation errors.
5. Do not change parts without errors.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

  public void addDepartment(final Department department)
  {
    if (department == null)
    {
      throw new IllegalArgumentException("Department cannot be null.");
    }
    String deptName = department.getName();
    if (deptName == null || deptName.isEmpty())
    {
      throw new IllegalArgumentException("Department name cannot be null or empty.");
    }
    if (getDepartments().size() >= 8)

    department.setCompany(this);

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
A company is comprised of two to eight departments. Each department has an ID and email. A department hires employees for certain projects. Employees working on projects can be temporary employees or permanent employees. Each employee is identified by a name, email, employee ID, employee number. Projects can be of types - production projects, research projects, education projects, and community projects. All projects have a title, description, budget amount, and deadline. In addition, each education project and community project are associated with one funding group. The funding group can be of types - private group, government group, or mixed group. The production projects are characterized by a site code.

functional requirement：
1.Total budget of all projects in a company.Calculate the sum of the budget amounts of all projects in all departments of a company. 

2.Average budget of all projects in a department.Calculate the average budget amount of all projects in a given department.

3.Count the distinct number of employees who work on at least one production projects in the company. An employee working on multiple production projects is counted only once.

4.Funding group type of all community projects in a department.Retrieve the funding group type of all community projects within a given department.

5.Manage departments in a company. Ensure that the company always has between 2 and 8 departments (inclusive). Allow adding a new department with unique name only if the current number of departments is less than 8, and allow removing an existing department by name only if the current number of departments is greater than 2; reject any operation that would violate these constraints. When the number of departments exceeds the maximum limit or falls below the minimum limit, throw an IllegalStateException; when the department name is duplicate, null, empty, or the target department to remove does not exist, throw an IllegalArgumentException.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title project Model

class CommunityProject {
    fundingGroup : FundingGroup
}
class Company {
    departments : List<Department>
    addDepartment(department : Department) : void
    calculateTotalBudget() : double
    countEmployeesInProductionProjects() : int
    removeDepartment(name : String) : String
}
class Department {
    ID : String
    email : String
    company : Company
    employees : List<Employee>
    projects : List<Project>
    calculateAverageBudget() : double
    getFundingGroupTypeCommunityProjects() : List<FundingGroupType>
}
class EducationProject {
    fundingGroup : FundingGroup
}
class Employee {
    ID : String
    email : String
    name : String
    number : String
    type : EmployeeType
    department : Department
}
class FundingGroup {
    name : String
    type : FundingGroupType
    communityProjects : List<CommunityProject>
    educationProjects : List<EducationProject>
}
class ProductionProject {
    siteCode : String
}
abstract class Project {
    budget : double
    deadline : EDate
    description : String
    title : String
    department : Department
    workingEmployees : List<Employee>
}
class ResearchProject {
}
enum EmployeeType {
    TEMPORARY
    PERMANENT
}
enum FundingGroupType {
    PRIVATE
    GOVERNMENT
    MIXED
}

CommunityProject --|> Project
CommunityProject --> FundingGroup : fundingGroup  'opposite: communityProjects
Company *-- "*" Department : departments  'opposite: company
Department --> Company : company  'opposite: departments
Department *-- "*" Employee : employees  'opposite: department
Department *-- "*" Project : projects  'opposite: department
EducationProject --|> Project
EducationProject --> FundingGroup : fundingGroup  'opposite: educationProjects
Employee --> Department : department  'opposite: employees
FundingGroup --> "*" CommunityProject : communityProjects  'opposite: fundingGroup
FundingGroup --> "*" EducationProject : educationProjects  'opposite: fundingGroup
ProductionProject --|> Project
Project --> Department : department  'opposite: projects
Project --> "*" Employee : workingEmployees
ResearchProject --|> Project
@enduml
```

   2. Initialize sum = 0.0.

# Requirement
```text
A company is comprised of two to eight departments. Each department has an ID and email. A department hires employees for certain projects. Employees working on projects can be temporary employees or permanent employees. Each employee is identified by a name, email, employee ID, employee number. Projects can be of types - production projects, research projects, education projects, and community projects. All projects have a title, description, budget amount, and deadline. In addition, each education project and community project are associated with one funding group. The funding group can be of types - private group, government group, or mixed group. The production projects are characterized by a site code.

functional requirement：
1.Total budget of all projects in a company.Calculate the sum of the budget amounts of all projects in all departments of a company. 

2.Average budget of all projects in a department.Calculate the average budget amount of all projects in a given department.

3.Count the distinct number of employees who work on at least one production projects in the company. An employee working on multiple production projects is counted only once.

4.Funding group type of all community projects in a department.Retrieve the funding group type of all community projects within a given department.

5.Manage departments in a company. Ensure that the company always has between 2 and 8 departments (inclusive). Allow adding a new department with unique name only if the current number of departments is less than 8, and allow removing an existing department by name only if the current number of departments is greater than 2; reject any operation that would violate these constraints. When the number of departments exceeds the maximum limit or falls below the minimum limit, throw an IllegalStateException; when the department name is duplicate, null, empty, or the target department to remove does not exist, throw an IllegalArgumentException.
```

# Operation Specifications
Department::calculateAverageBudget
1. Summary: Calculates the arithmetic mean of the budget amounts of all projects assigned to the department.
2. Algorithm:
   1. If this.projects is null or empty, return 0.0.
   2. Initialize sum = 0.0.
   3. For each project in this.projects:
      a. sum += project.budget.
   4. Calculate average = sum / this.projects.size().
   5. Return average.
3. Related Features: projects
4. Related Classes: Project, List, double
5. Input: None
6. Output: double: The average budget of projects within the department. Value range: >= 0.0.
7. Pre-condition: The Department instance and its 'projects' collection are initialized.
8. Post-condition: Returns the precise average budget. System state remains unchanged. Division-by-zero is safely handled by returning 0.0 for empty project lists.

Department::getFundingGroupTypeCommunityProjects
1. Summary: Retrieves the funding group types associated with all community projects located within the department.
2. Algorithm:
   1. Initialize List<FundingGroupType> resultTypes = new ArrayList<>();
   2. For each project in this.projects:
      a. If project instanceof CommunityProject:
         i. Retrieve project.fundingGroup.
         ii. Append fundingGroup.type to resultTypes.
   3. Return resultTypes.
3. Related Features: projects
4. Related Classes: Project, CommunityProject, FundingGroup, FundingGroupType, List
5. Input: None
6. Output: List<FundingGroupType>: A list containing the funding group types (PRIVATE, GOVERNMENT, or MIXED) corresponding to each community project in the department. Duplicates are preserved if multiple projects share the same funding group type.
7. Pre-condition: The Department instance and its 'projects' collection are initialized.
8. Post-condition: Returns the collected funding group types. System state remains unchanged.

# Operation Specifications
Company::calculateTotalBudget
1. Summary: Computes the sum of budget amounts for all projects belonging to all departments within the company.
2. Algorithm:
   1. Initialize totalBudget = 0.0.
   2. For each dept in this.departments:
      a. For each project in dept.projects:
         i. totalBudget += project.budget.
   3. Return totalBudget.
3. Related Features: departments
4. Related Classes: Department, Project, List, double
5. Input: None
6. Output: double: The aggregate budget amount across all projects in the company. Value range: >= 0.0.
7. Pre-condition: The company and its associated department/project collections are initialized. Collections may be empty.
8. Post-condition: Returns the exact summation of all project budgets. System state remains completely unchanged.

Company::addDepartment
1. Summary: Adds a new department to the company while enforcing constraints on the maximum department count and name uniqueness.
2. Algorithm:
   1. If this.departments.size() >= 8, throw IllegalStateException.
   2. Validate department.name: if null, empty, or already exists in this.departments, throw IllegalArgumentException.
   3. Add department to this.departments.
   4. Set department.company = this.
   5. Return void.
3. Related Features: departments, name (inferred)
4. Related Classes: Department, String, List<Department>
5. Input: department (Department): The new department instance to be added. Must contain a valid, non-null, non-empty 'name' attribute that is not already registered in the company.
6. Output: void
7. Pre-condition: The Company instance is properly initialized. The system is in a valid state.
8. Post-condition: The 'departments' list contains the new department. The 'company' reference of the added department points to this Company. Total departments <= 8, and all department names remain unique.

Company::countEmployeesInProductionProjects
1. Summary: Counts the distinct number of employees who are assigned to at least one production project across the entire company.
2. Algorithm:
   1. Initialize Set<Employee> distinctEmployees = new HashSet<>();
   2. For each dept in this.departments:
      a. For each project in dept.projects:
         i. If project instanceof ProductionProject:
            - For each emp in project.workingEmployees:
               * distinctEmployees.add(emp).
   3. Return distinctEmployees.size().
3. Related Features: departments
4. Related Classes: Department, Project, ProductionProject, Employee, Set, int
5. Input: None
6. Output: int: The count of unique employees working on production projects. Value range: >= 0.
7. Pre-condition: The company and its data structures are initialized.
8. Post-condition: Returns the accurate count of distinct employees involved in production projects. System state remains unchanged.

Company::removeDepartment
1. Summary: Removes an existing department from the company by its name, enforcing minimum count and existence constraints.
2. Algorithm:
   1. Locate department in this.departments where department.name equals input name.
   2. If not found, throw IllegalArgumentException.
   3. If this.departments.size() <= 2, throw IllegalStateException.
   4. Remove the located department from this.departments.
   5. Set department.company = null.
   6. Return "Department [name] removed successfully.".
3. Related Features: departments
4. Related Classes: Department, String, List<Department>

# Requirement
```text
A company is comprised of two to eight departments. Each department has an ID and email. A department hires employees for certain projects. Employees working on projects can be temporary employees or permanent employees. Each employee is identified by a name, email, employee ID, employee number. Projects can be of types - production projects, research projects, education projects, and community projects. All projects have a title, description, budget amount, and deadline. In addition, each education project and community project are associated with one funding group. The funding group can be of types - private group, government group, or mixed group. The production projects are characterized by a site code.

functional requirement：
1.Total budget of all projects in a company.Calculate the sum of the budget amounts of all projects in all departments of a company. 

2.Average budget of all projects in a department.Calculate the average budget amount of all projects in a given department.

3.Count the distinct number of employees who work on at least one production projects in the company. An employee working on multiple production projects is counted only once.

4.Funding group type of all community projects in a department.Retrieve the funding group type of all community projects within a given department.

5.Manage departments in a company. Ensure that the company always has between 2 and 8 departments (inclusive). Allow adding a new department with unique name only if the current number of departments is less than 8, and allow removing an existing department by name only if the current number of departments is greater than 2; reject any operation that would violate these constraints. When the number of departments exceeds the maximum limit or falls below the minimum limit, throw an IllegalStateException; when the department name is duplicate, null, empty, or the target department to remove does not exist, throw an IllegalArgumentException.
```

# Operation Specifications
Company::calculateTotalBudget
1. Summary: Computes the sum of budget amounts for all projects belonging to all departments within the company.
2. Algorithm:
   1. Initialize totalBudget = 0.0.
   2. For each dept in this.departments:
      a. For each project in dept.projects:
         i. totalBudget += project.budget.
   3. Return totalBudget.
3. Related Features: departments
4. Related Classes: Department, Project, List, double
5. Input: None
6. Output: double: The aggregate budget amount across all projects in the company. Value range: >= 0.0.
7. Pre-condition: The company and its associated department/project collections are initialized. Collections may be empty.
8. Post-condition: Returns the exact summation of all project budgets. System state remains completely unchanged.

Company::addDepartment
1. Summary: Adds a new department to the company while enforcing constraints on the maximum department count and name uniqueness.
2. Algorithm:
   1. If this.departments.size() >= 8, throw IllegalStateException.
   2. Validate department.name: if null, empty, or already exists in this.departments, throw IllegalArgumentException.
   3. Add department to this.departments.
   4. Set department.company = this.
   5. Return void.
3. Related Features: departments, name (inferred)
4. Related Classes: Department, String, List<Department>
5. Input: department (Department): The new department instance to be added. Must contain a valid, non-null, non-empty 'name' attribute that is not already registered in the company.
6. Output: void
7. Pre-condition: The Company instance is properly initialized. The system is in a valid state.
8. Post-condition: The 'departments' list contains the new department. The 'company' reference of the added department points to this Company. Total departments <= 8, and all department names remain unique.

Company::countEmployeesInProductionProjects
1. Summary: Counts the distinct number of employees who are assigned to at least one production project across the entire company.
2. Algorithm:
   1. Initialize Set<Employee> distinctEmployees = new HashSet<>();
   2. For each dept in this.departments:
      a. For each project in dept.projects:
         i. If project instanceof ProductionProject:
            - For each emp in project.workingEmployees:
               * distinctEmployees.add(emp).
   3. Return distinctEmployees.size().
3. Related Features: departments
4. Related Classes: Department, Project, ProductionProject, Employee, Set, int
5. Input: None
6. Output: int: The count of unique employees working on production projects. Value range: >= 0.
7. Pre-condition: The company and its data structures are initialized.
8. Post-condition: Returns the accurate count of distinct employees involved in production projects. System state remains unchanged.

Company::removeDepartment
1. Summary: Removes an existing department from the company by its name, enforcing minimum count and existence constraints.
2. Algorithm:
   1. Locate department in this.departments where department.name equals input name.
   2. If not found, throw IllegalArgumentException.
   3. If this.departments.size() <= 2, throw IllegalStateException.
   4. Remove the located department from this.departments.
   5. Set department.company = null.
   6. Return "Department [name] removed successfully.".
3. Related Features: departments
4. Related Classes: Department, String, List<Department>
5. Input: name (String): The identifier of the department to be removed. Must exactly match an existing department name in the company.
6. Output: String: A status confirmation message indicating successful removal.
7. Pre-condition: The target department exists in the company, and the current number of departments is strictly greater than 2.
8. Post-condition: The specified department is removed from the 'departments' list. Its 'company' reference is nullified. Total departments decreases by 1 and remains >= 2.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.2 Locate these files in your project.
  1.3 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the context.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

  public double calculateAverageBudget()
  {
     if (getProjects() == null || getProjects().isEmpty()) {
        return 0.0;
     }
     double sum = 0.0;
     for (Project project : getProjects()) {
        sum += project.getBudget();
     }
     return sum / getProjects().size();
  }
